 status COMPLETE = every question/sub-question of the paper was read against its mark-scheme entry by the reviewing agent and passes the mechanical checks below;

 Paper 2 mechanical checks: (1) every leaf sub-question printed in the QP with a [n] mark allocation has a findings line; (2) for every in-scope
        sub-question the mark tags written in the findings (e.g. [B1] [C1] [A1]) add up to the QP mark allocation; (3) QP/MS headers carry the same component and session.
 Paper 1 mechanical checks: all 40 MCQs have a findings line; the agent's independently worked answer equals the official key (or the disagreement is documented).
 section codes: syllabus subsection numbers; codes starting with X are outside the requested scope (X1.8 pressure, X2 thermal, X4.1/4.3/4.4/4.5/4.6, X5 nuclear, X6 space, XOLD legacy topics)
 a code joined with '+' (e.g. 4.2.4+X4.3) marks a sub-question of a selected section that also needs a step from another section: the record keeps all its marking points,
        the notes use only the in-scope steps (wording in tools/notes_src/_overrides.txt). Section numbers and content follow the syllabus for 2026-2028; series and parallel circuit rules are X4.3.
 {F} = figure/graph/diagram inspected visually for this item; {M} = mark-scheme page inspected visually.
 Paper 1 records printed as '(identical text to <pair> Q<n>)' are questions repeated in another variant or session (535 of the 3120 questions). Each was compared with the question it repeats
        (521 character-identical, 14 identical apart from punctuation or typography; official keys equal in every case) and is covered through that already-verified question.
 notes updated = yes when every in-scope findings line of the pair is filed in a sub-topic of physics_papers_1_2_notes.txt that has a checklist (checked by tools/audit_notes.py).

    Q3 key B | worked B | 1.2+1.7.1 :: {F} freewheeling with no friction: the speed is greatest at the bottom of a hill, where the gravitational potential energy is least, so the first peak of the speed–time graph marks the bottom of the first hill
    Q4 key C | worked C | 1.5.2 :: a light ball dropped in air: velocity increases while acceleration decreases (air resistance grows as the speed rises)
    Q5 key C | worked C | 1.5.2 :: {F} as a falling object speeds up, the upward air resistance increases, so the resultant force (weight − air resistance) decreases until it is zero at constant speed
    Q6 key A | worked A | 1.5.4+1.5.2 :: {F} the force that makes a motorcyclist move round a corner is the sideways friction of the road on the tyres, directed towards the centre of the turn
    Q7 key A | worked A | 1.5.5 :: {F} moments about the pivot: 3 × 6 = P × 2 + 4 × 2, so P = 5 N (an upward force on the same side as a downward force turns the beam the opposite way)
    Q8 key C | worked C | 1.5.6 :: {F} the centre of mass is nearest the base in the shape that has most of its area (mass) close to the base
    Q9 key C | worked C | 1.5.3 :: extension is proportional to load below the limit of proportionality: 4 mm for 2 N means 10 mm for a total of 5 N; length = 1000 + 10 = 1010 mm
    Q10 key D | worked D | 1.4 :: volume of one clip = mass ÷ density = 0.50 ÷ 8.0 = 0.0625 cm³; number = 20 ÷ 0.0625 = 320

    Q22 key D | worked D | 3.2.1 :: {F} angles of incidence and reflection are measured from the normal: a ray at 40° to the mirror surface has i = r = 90° − 40° = 50°
    Q23 key B | worked B | 3.2.2 :: {F} the critical angle is the angle of incidence in the glass, between the ray and the normal, for which the refracted ray travels along the surface
    Q24 key C | worked C | 3.2.3 :: {F} short sight: rays from a distant object are brought to a focus in front of the retina

    Q28 key C | worked C | 4.2.4 :: {F} I–V graphs: a straight line through the origin for a resistor at constant temperature; a curve that bends towards the voltage axis (flattening) for a filament lamp
    Q29 key A | worked A | 4.2.4 :: {F} I = V ÷ R = 10 ÷ 2000 = 0.005 A = 5 mA (kΩ converted to Ω)

  note: P1 w11_12 | main sheets 1-4 and rest sheet viewed; zoom viewed for Q10, 21, 33, 34, 36; Q26 is identical to s18_12 Q34
    Q1 key C | worked C | 1.5.2 :: {F} terminal velocity is reached when a falling object is still moving but no longer accelerating (air resistance equals weight)
    Q2 key C | worked C | 1.5.1 :: {F} Newton's third law: if the propeller pushes water backwards with 2000 N, the water pushes the propeller forwards with an equal force of 2000 N (the resistive force is not part of this pair)
    Q3 key B | worked B | 1.2 :: {F} distance = area under the speed–time graph = (4 × 5) + ½ × (4 + 6) × 2 = 20 + 10 = 30 m
    Q4 key B | worked B | 1.5.1+1.5.2 :: {F} when the link breaks the truck has only resistive forces acting, so it slows down gradually; the engine has the same driving force but less resistance, so there is a resultant forward force and it speeds up
    Q5 key B | worked B | 1.3 :: {F} a newton meter measures weight, which is different on the Moon; a pan balance compares masses, so its reading is the same on the Earth and on the Moon
    Q6 key A | worked A | 1.1 :: a micrometer measures the diameter of a thin wire; a (metre) rule measures a length of about 1 m

  Q11  [QP p12-12 ↔ MS p8-8]
    11aiii [9m] 1.7.1 :: {F} roller-coaster energy changes: going downhill the gravitational potential energy decreases [B1] ;; and kinetic energy is gained [B1] ;; going uphill kinetic energy changes back to potential energy [B1] ;; maximum potential energy = mgh [C1] ;; 500 × 10 × 30 [C1] ;; 150 000 J [A1] ;; with no friction, conservation of energy: the loss in potential energy equals the gain in kinetic energy [C1] ;; K.E. at the lower point = mg × height fallen = 500 × 10 × (30 − 10) [C1] ;; 100 000 J [A1]
    11bii [3m] 1.5.4+1.1 :: {F} velocity is a vector – it includes direction; speed does not [B1] ;; on a bend the direction of motion changes, so the velocity changes even at constant speed [B1] ;; the force making the carriage move round the curve acts towards the centre of the curve (inwards) [B1]

    Q3 key C | worked C | 1.2 :: {F} distance travelled while the speed is increasing = area under that part of the speed–time graph = ½ × 20 × 10 = 100 m
    Q4 key D | worked D | 1.3 :: gravitational field strength = weight ÷ mass
    Q5 key C | worked C | 1.4 :: {F} volume of the ball = rise in the water level = 40 − 30 = 10 cm³; mass = increase in the balance reading = 180 − 100 = 80 g; density = 80 ÷ 10 = 8.0 g/cm³
    Q6 key D | worked D | 1.5.1 :: Newton's third law: to every action there is an equal but opposite reaction
    Q7 key C | worked C | 1.5.2 :: oil reduces the friction between the tyres and the road, so the car takes longer to slow down (the braking distance increases; the driver's thinking distance is unchanged)
    Q8 key A | worked A | 1.5.5 :: {F} moment of a force about a pivot = force × perpendicular distance from the pivot to the line of action of the force (F × d), not the distance along the pole, up the wall or along the chain
    Q9 key D | worked D | 1.5.3 :: (identical text to w07_1 Q8) {F} extension = length − unloaded length (2 cm at zero load); an extension of 3 cm means a length of 5 cm, which the graph gives at 15 N
    Q10 key A | worked A | 1.1 :: {F} the resultant of two forces acting at a point is the diagonal of the parallelogram (rectangle) drawn from that point, with its arrow pointing away from the point
    Q11 key C | worked C | XOLD :: mass–energy equivalence

    Q20 key C | worked C | 3.2.1 :: {F} for a plane mirror the angle of incidence equals the angle of reflection, both measured between the ray and the normal (not between the ray and the mirror), and the image is as far behind the mirror as the object is in front
    Q21 key A | worked A | 3.2.2 :: (identical text to w11_12 Q20) {F} sin i against sin r is a straight line through the origin with gradient n (greater than 1 for light entering glass), so sin i reaches 1 while sin r is still about 0.65
    Q22 key D | worked D | 3.2.3 :: {F} for an object closer to a converging lens than the principal focus, the refracted rays diverge and never meet; traced back they appear to come from a virtual, upright, enlarged image on the same side as the object, further from the lens than the object
    Q23 key C | worked C | 3.2.4 :: {F} dispersion by a prism begins at the first face (the colours separate inside the glass) and increases at the second face; red is deviated least and violet most
    Q24 key A | worked A | 3.4 :: {F} sound is a longitudinal wave: in one period (1/200 s for 200 Hz) an air molecule moves forwards and back again along the direction in which the sound travels; it does not travel along with the wave
